Removing NGS measurements produces a −5.90% jet result and a −5.17% simple-kinematic result. The five-front-drill selected-tracking experiment gives +4.34%, with an interval crossing zero. No scouting edge is established by any of these variants.

The matched-movement result is an interesting lead, not the main claim. Simple kinematics improve this target by 1.63% in 2024 and 40.28% in 2025; the pooled gain is heavily concentrated in the latter class. Removing NGS gives a similar pooled point gain of 24.31%. However, 32 complete original-player refits expand the simple-kinematic interval to −23.97% through +34.80%. The primary jet get-off refits give −50.57% through +24.30%. All 32 requested draws succeeded in each study. These small empirical percentile studies measure procedure instability; they are neither prospective confidence guarantees nor correction for the entire history of adaptive research.

There is also a model-selection lesson. The inner-selected movement family is not necessarily the best family on the small outer class. Publishing the best outer family as though it had been selected prospectively would turn an exploratory lead into a misleading headline. The release keeps both the fixed family contrasts and the actual inner-selected procedure.

## A measured low-noise representation across repeated attempts

The follow-up uses one three-coordinate area-acceleration vector per distinct forty attempt. The coordinates are the three overlapping speed gates, so their covariance is estimated jointly. For a working model \(z_{ij}=\mu+u_i+\epsilon_{ij}\), pooled within-player differences estimate \(N=\operatorname{Cov}(\epsilon)\). Player-mean covariance, after subtracting the average \(N/n_i\) contribution, estimates the between-player signal \(S\). A fixed 5% trace-scaled ridge stabilizes the noise covariance.

The generalized eigenproblem \(Sq=\lambda Nq\) distinguishes repeatable directions from noise-dominated directions. Negative empirical signal eigenvalues are reported and clipped for this estimator; they are not asserted to prove that a biological direction is absent. The two earlier-class calibrations have spectra approximately \((-0.0235,0.3424,5.5431)\) and \((-0.0474,0.2131,3.2461)\). Both concentrate their positive noise-whitened signal strongly in one direction, with a weaker second direction. That is a reason to shrink uncertain coordinates, not to claim an exact two-dimensional physiological state.

The posterior mean is returned in the original physical coordinates:

\[
\widehat z_i=\mu+S(S+N/n_i)^{-1}(\bar z_i-\mu).
\]

This matters because eigenvectors can rotate or change sign across training folds. Feeding differently oriented latent coordinates into one shared decoder would silently change the meaning of a feature. The implementation rebuilds the covariance model inside each fold but maps every output back to the same three physical acceleration coordinates. Its covariance is conditional on the working model and estimated parameters; it is not a calibrated uncertainty interval for NFL ability.

## Strong independent-attempt controls

Using earlier classes to calibrate and the first distinct attempt to predict the second gives 55 evaluated source players: 26 from 2024 and 29 from 2025. The noise-aware estimator has MSE 0.17290, compared with 0.22417 for the raw first attempt. This is the 22.87% improvement. Sixty-four complete original-player source refits all succeed, with a development interval of +13.13% to +29.70% against raw observations.

That comparison alone is insufficient to credit sophisticated mathematics. The follow-up adds a pooled prior, a training-selected scalar shrinkage, diagonal reliability shrinkage and multivariate ridge with five training-selected penalties. Both orientations of a training repeat pair stay together in the same original-player fold; augmentation is not counted as extra independent people. Every control uses only source data from earlier classes.

| Noise-aware estimator compared with | MSE gain | Full-refit 90% development interval |
|---|---:|---:|
| Raw first attempt | +22.87% | +13.13% to +29.70% |
| Pooled prior | +34.20% | +20.46% to +50.94% |
| Scalar shrinkage | +8.39% | −8.15% to +15.19% |
| Diagonal reliability shrinkage | +4.15% | −4.74% to +14.00% |
| Multivariate ridge | −1.70% | −4.62% to +5.10% |

The properly tuned ordinary ridge is slightly better on this retained test. The defensible finding is that denoising helps repeated measurement, not that the covariance method beats strong conventional prediction. Its additional value is an interpretable noise decomposition, physical-coordinate transport and an explicit conditional uncertainty model. Transporting the reliable acceleration coordinates to NFL get-off gives −0.24% versus the conventional baseline; the raw three-coordinate acceleration variant gives −4.17%. The refinement mitigates overfitting, but still does not create an incremental scouting advantage. Without NGS the reliable get-off result is −4.08%.

## What the bridges now accomplish

The earlier mathematical programs enter as executable mechanisms rather than decorative vocabulary. Observability supplies the exact hidden-launch witness and asks for a recoverable target instead. Ordered-path algebra isolates weighted acceleration from constant position and velocity. Exact rational readout design supplies the derivative weights, minimum-noise solution and nullspace obstruction. Statistical state reduction learns which correlated attempt coordinates warrant shrinkage, while preserving physical coordinates across splits. The empirical comparison identifies where the chain still fails: usable source measurement and repeatability do not yet translate into a dependable get-off advantage.

This is not evidence that acceleration never matters in football. It is evidence against deploying these fitted scouting corrections on this sample. A plausible next physical hypothesis is that launch timing, assignment and the transfer from motion to a football task matter more than another high-order derivative. That interpretation remains a hypothesis; the current experiments do not identify its causal contribution. The next confirmatory study needs genuinely uninspected players, a declared motor endpoint and get-off endpoint kept separate, and a strong ordinary history/reliability control. A missing-launch clip should be valued for the rolling readouts it actually observes, not for a launch time the data cannot uniquely supply.

## Reproduction and retained evidence

Install `.[scouting]` from the repository. First reproduce the owned checkpoint with the existing nine-file preparation script, or reuse the already-owned hash-matching checkpoint. Do not deserialize a checkpoint from an untrusted source; a hash establishes equality, not the safety of arbitrary pickle bytes. The four new runners require both the checkpoint and its preparation receipt, and require restricted individual outputs outside the public repository.

```bash
python scripts/run_stopped_flow.py --checkpoint PRIVATE/official_bridge_data.pkl --preparation results/real-nfl/preparation.json --out results/stopped-flow --private PRIVATE/stopped-flow --refits 32
python scripts/run_motor_sensitivity.py --checkpoint PRIVATE/official_bridge_data.pkl --preparation results/real-nfl/preparation.json --out results/stopped-flow --private PRIVATE/stopped-flow --refits 32
python scripts/run_reliable_stopped_flow.py --checkpoint PRIVATE/official_bridge_data.pkl --preparation results/real-nfl/preparation.json --out results/stopped-flow --private PRIVATE/stopped-flow
python scripts/run_repeat_controls.py --checkpoint PRIVATE/official_bridge_data.pkl --preparation results/real-nfl/preparation.json --out results/stopped-flow --private PRIVATE/stopped-flow --refits 64
python -m unittest discover -s tests -v
```

The retained study contains 8,568 candidate outcome-regression fits: 600 across five initial/sensitivity experiments, 288 across three reliability experiments, and 7,680 across 64 complete NFL procedure refits. This count excludes additional context-normalization fits and does not treat repeated runs as new evidence. The strong source-repeat comparison adds 2,080 ridge fits across its initial run and 64 complete refits. The complete source archive includes exact kernels, all previous mathematical content, tests, this chapter, protocols, aggregate receipts and a source-complete notebook. Restricted input and individual derived predictions remain in the separate private checkpoint package. There is no new Lean formalization claim and no claimed live-site or upstream-application deployment.


---

# PerfectJet Exact Observatory

PerfectJet Exact Observatory turns a mathematical system into something one can question, compress, navigate and interrogate. Supply states, actions, observations, a target and an acceptance condition. The same executable model then answers several connected questions: which states have identical futures, how many constrained histories exist, which history occupies a chosen rank, what the accepted population does at a remote length, and which experiment reveals a hidden target at the least worst-case cost. Its central design principle is **to preserve exactly the information needed by the question**. Changing the question can change the appropriate representation dramatically. This release implements that principle through a functioning local workbench, reusable Python classes, a JSON batch interface, independent benchmark calculations and an arithmetic bridge from an unbounded Pell orbit.

The main arithmetic laboratory has 2,197 original states. Its complete label-preserving behavioral model has 85 states. Exact numeric sensor evolution needs 57 linear coordinates; ordered acceptance readouts need 15; counting histories by length needs an eight-dimensional global Krylov space; the designated starting state's scalar count has a minimal recurrence of order seven. These are different mathematical objects with different preservation promises. The sequence 2,197 → 85 → 57 → 15 → 8 → 7 expresses how the information demand determines the computational burden. It does not assert that seven states reproduce the entire original machine. The workbench makes the transitions, quotient fibers, histories, experiment policies and calculation records inspectable together.

Python 3.10 or later is sufficient for this release's core. From the repository directory, install the package and launch the local interface with the following commands. The first command installs the local source; the second starts a server on the computer's loopback interface and opens its browser page. The alternate installed command is `perfectjet observatory --open`. The page provides generated mathematical examples, a model editor and JSON import, exact population controls, rank navigation, uniform sampling, an animated route, experiment design, remote coefficient queries and downloadable records. Its moving graph shows the computed finite model and selected history; it is a view of actual model transitions.

```bash
python -m pip install -e .
python -m perfectjet.observatory.workbench --open
```

An extracted release also runs without package installation: execute `python run_observatory.py` from its directory, or double-click `Start_Observatory.bat` on Windows. These launchers use the included source and open the same local workbench. Python 3.10 or later must already be available. The server prints its local address, and Ctrl+C stops it.

The source of truth is the Python implementation. `src/perfectjet/observatory/model.py` validates models and compiles their behavioral quotients; `histories.py` handles constrained populations; `series.py` constructs action-closed observable spaces and exact recurrences; `experiments.py` solves pair and adaptive interrogation problems; `arithmetic.py` transports seeded Pell orbits into finite residue systems. `examples.py` generates the example specifications. `workbench.py` presents those computations through HTTP, and `command.py` exposes the same request router for batch use. A JSON request containing `operation` and `model`, with the operation's parameters, can be evaluated using `python -m perfectjet.observatory.command request.json --output answer.json`. Exporting a model and a calculation record from the page supplies concrete material for reproduction.

The underlying finite model consists of a nonempty state set S, a finite ordered action alphabet, positive integer action costs, a partial deterministic transition Fₐ for each action, a sensor label, a target label and a terminal acceptance flag at each state. Labels are finite JSON scalars, with Boolean and integer identities kept distinct. Undefined transitions are explicit. A history is an action word, so two different actions remain different choices even when they reach the same state. This distinction is essential for exact populations: replacing two parallel labeled actions with one unlabeled edge would change the answer. Costs belong to actions and accumulate along the word. Sensor-trace constraints can include the initial reading as well as every subsequent reading.

Behavioral compilation begins by separating states with different sensor, target or acceptance labels, or different action definedness. It then splits blocks whenever an action sends their members into different existing blocks. The implementation uses inverse transition lists and a splitter worklist, avoiding a preliminary all-pairs search for distinguishing words. At termination, the quotient map π satisfies π(Fₐ(s)) = F̄ₐ(π(s)) whenever the transition exists, and preserves undefinedness and all retained labels. This equation is checked for every original state and action by `replay_compilation`. Starting from equal quotient states and applying induction on word length proves equal definedness and equal retained outputs after every finite action word. Thus every quotient history corresponds to the same labeled action word in the original coordinates.

The converse supplies minimality for these retained labels. An initial separation already has an immediate label or definedness witness. Every later separation has an action leading to a previously separated pair of blocks. Prepending that action to the previous witness gives a finite distinguishing word. Therefore two states remain together exactly when no finite word distinguishes their specified future behavior. The quotient is a minimal deterministic representation under that equivalence, including unreachable states unless the supplied model itself omits them. Preserving current target labels throughout this quotient is a stronger requirement than merely preserving terminal acceptance. The adaptive experiment solver has its own target semantics: it explicitly remembers the target of the initial hidden state even after destructive actions.

The interferometer example uses arithmetic modulo 13. Absolute states are triples (x,y,z), with relative coordinates u=x−z and v=y−z. The sensor is q=u²+v² modulo 13, represented by its integer residue from zero to twelve. Action A maps (u,v) to (u+v,v), and B maps it to (u,v+u). A transition whose next sensor is zero is blocked. Every terminal state is marked accepting; consequently the safety restriction is enforced by transition definedness. The designated initial state is (1,0,0), whose sensor is one. Counts from this safe start require every subsequent reading to remain nonzero. This convention does not declare arbitrary zero-sensor starting states safe, and it explains why global representations can retain modes absent from the designated start.

Adding the same phase to x, y and z leaves u and v unchanged. Simultaneously negating u and v leaves the sensor and all future sensors unchanged because both shear maps respect that sign symmetry. There are 169 relative states and (169+1)/2 = 85 sign orbits. The benchmark checks the complete computed partition against this independently derived translation-and-sign relation, rather than checking only its size. The fiber over the relative zero orbit has thirteen absolute states; each other fiber has twenty-six. From the designated safe start, only 72 relative states and 36 quotient states are reachable. The 85-state quotient and the global linear dimensions refer to the complete supplied model, so they should not be confused with those reachable-state counts.

Action order remains visible after symmetry reduction. Starting at (u,v)=(1,0), applying A then B ends at (1,1) and gives q=2. Applying B then A ends at (2,1) and gives q=5. Both words contain one A and one B. Their action histograms agree while their outputs differ. This concrete witness shows why a commutative inventory of actions cannot replace the ordered model. Pair experiments can also separate two states that currently have the same sensor when their future readings diverge, while antipodal relative states remain indistinguishable under every finite word. The workbench's orbit picture therefore represents a dynamical equivalence, rather than a visual grouping by current color.

For an exact horizon h, remaining budget b and starting state s, let C(h,s,b) be the number of accepted continuations satisfying the requested sensor trace. A mismatched current trace entry contributes zero. At h=0 the value is the terminal acceptance flag. Otherwise, C is the sum of C(h−1,Fₐ(s),b−cost(a)) over defined, affordable actions. The unconstrained-budget version omits b. The recurrence is exact because every nonempty word has one first action and a unique suffix, and the first-action subsets are disjoint. Equal target states do not merge those subsets. Memoization shares repeated continuation questions, while an explicit evaluation stack avoids recursion-depth dependence. Sufficiently generous budgets are removed as redundant coordinates.

Ranking refines the same decomposition. In the original action order, all histories beginning with the first action occupy one interval of size equal to that action's continuation count; the next action occupies the following interval, and so on. To rank a valid word, sum the intervals belonging to earlier first actions and recursively rank its suffix. To invert a rank, find its containing interval, subtract the interval's starting position and continue. Induction on h proves that these procedures are inverse bijections between accepted words and the integers from zero through C−1. Uniform sampling therefore needs only a uniform integer in that interval followed by inverse ranking: every accepted word has exactly one preimage and probability 1/C. This avoids sampling unconstrained words and rejecting nearly all of them when valid histories are rare.

The retained benchmark records the following exact counts from the designated interferometer start. The right column gives every binary action word before the blocked-transition constraint is applied. These populations become sharply different even at moderate lengths, while the count recurrence remains small.

| Length | Accepted histories | All binary words |
|---:|---:|---:|
| 10 | 162 | 1,024 |
| 20 | 20,010 | 1,048,576 |
| 40 | 311,989,738 | 1,099,511,627,776 |
| 80 | 76,712,911,076,419,434 | 1,208,925,819,614,629,174,706,176 |

Linear observable compression asks a more specific question than behavioral equivalence. Regard an observable f as a function on all quotient states. Define its action pullback by (Tₐf)(s)=f(Fₐ(s)), assigning zero at an undefined transition. Starting with acceptance and any explicitly supplied numeric observations, the compiler repeatedly adds action pullbacks that are linearly independent over the rational numbers. Finite dimension guarantees termination. Its basis matrix C and reduced action matrices Bₐ satisfy C[:,Fₐ(s)] = BₐC[:,s], with the zero vector for an undefined transition. Exact reconstruction of every retained seed observable and induction over ordered words prove preservation of every requested future readout. Basis-source words are recorded, so each basis function can also be replayed directly from its originating observable.

For the interferometer, including numeric q and acceptance produces dimension 57; including acceptance alone produces dimension 15. These are exact rational dimensions of functions on the finite state set, not numerical ranks from singular-value thresholds and not ranks over the field of thirteen elements. The 57-coordinate representation recovers the integer residue reading q after every ordered word. The 15-coordinate representation recovers the accepted-word readout but makes no promise to reconstruct q. Because acceptance is one at every live state in this example, its pullbacks also reveal whether a word remains defined. The benchmark replays 6,120 basis-source evaluations across these two closures and checks every reduced operator identity. Choosing an observable is consequently a precise specification of which information may be discarded.

Length counting discards another distinction: it sums over every action word of the same length. Write A=ΣₐTₐ and let f be the acceptance indicator. Then vₖ=Aᵏf is a whole-state vector whose entry at s counts accepted words of length k from s. Incremental exact elimination finds the first dependence vᵣ=Σⱼcⱼvⱼ. All earlier vectors are independent, making r the global Krylov dimension. Multiplying the identity by Aᵐ proves the recurrence for every subsequent length and every starting state. `compile_series` checks this vector identity explicitly; it does not infer an all-time law merely because a fitted recurrence matches a finite prefix. The recorded residual has one coordinate per state and is identically zero.

In the interferometer, r=8 and the lag coefficients are (1,0,0,2,−2,0,8,0). Thus the global recurrence is aₙ=aₙ₋₁+2aₙ₋₄−2aₙ₋₅+8aₙ₋₇ for n≥8, with its formally retained eighth lag equal to zero. For the designated start, the first values are 1,2,4,6,10,14,22,34, and the generating function is P(z)/Q(z), where

\[
P(z)=1+z+2z^2+2z^3+2z^4+2z^5+4z^6,
\qquad
Q(z)=1-z-2z^4+2z^5-8z^7.
\]

Eight describes the whole-state vector space; seven describes this selected scalar sequence. The global characteristic relation has a zero mode whose contribution vanishes at the designated safe start. Here the scalar recurrence already works from n=7. The benchmark independently computes the polynomial greatest common divisor of P and Q and obtains one; because the rational function is proper and reduced, its scalar denominator degree seven is minimal. The general library reports its global recurrence and explicitly avoids claiming that it has minimized every selected scalar output. This distinction matters whenever unreachable or unobserved modes disappear upon evaluation at a particular state.

Remote queries use the characteristic relation as a polynomial reduction rule. Reduce xᴺ modulo xʳ−Σⱼcⱼxʲ by binary powering; if the remainder is Σⱼdⱼxʲ, the same relation gives \(a_N=\sum_jd_ja_j\). There are O(r² log N) arithmetic operations with ordinary polynomial multiplication. Applying every operation modulo M keeps intermediate integers bounded by that modulus, including when M is composite. At length 10¹⁰⁰, the interferometer count modulo 1,000,000,007 is **976,449,876**. An independent modular matrix-power calculation on separately derived reachable sign orbits agrees. An exact unmodulated answer can require an enormous number of bits; jumping over earlier indices cannot remove the cost of representing the answer itself.

Experiment design introduces a different kind of compression: uncertainty about the current hidden state. A belief stores pairs (initial state,current state), retaining the initial target label through every transition. An undefined transition moves its hypothesis to an absorbing undefined observation; it does not silently remove that possibility. Free sensor readings partition the belief into observation branches. A branch is identified when every remaining initial hypothesis has the same target. The solver minimizes worst-case accumulated positive action cost. For a nonterminal belief B its Bellman equation is V(B)=minₐ[cost(a)+maxₒV(Bₐ,ₒ)], with identified beliefs assigned zero. This is a guaranteed identification objective; no prior probability or average-case information score is substituted for it.

The adaptive algorithm builds the reachable belief graph, then runs a reverse AND/OR form of Dijkstra's algorithm. An action offers a finite candidate only after all its distinct observation-successor beliefs have finalized values. Its candidate is its cost plus their maximum. A priority queue finalizes the smallest available candidate. To prove exactness, suppose the first incorrect finalization were more expensive than an optimal finite policy. Positive action costs make every child of that optimal first action strictly cheaper than its parent. Those children would already have finalized correctly, so the optimal candidate would already be in the queue, a contradiction. Chosen policy edges strictly decrease V, yielding a finite shared decision DAG. If complete exploration leaves an unresolved region, every action has an observation successor still inside it; this closed ambiguity region witnesses the absence of a finite guaranteed policy.

The branching laboratory demonstrates a strict adaptive advantage. An unknown device belongs to family alpha or beta and holds a hidden bit. Routing first reveals the family; inspecting that family then reveals the bit. The exact worst-case cost is two. Choosing the wrong family's inspection makes the relevant hypotheses undefined without revealing their bit, and both retain their original targets. An independent complete search through eight preset-word monitors, including their full observation histories, finds no fixed action word that identifies the bit in all cases. A separate irreversible-button example assigns cost three to inspection and cost one to reset. Reset merges both initial bit possibilities into the same current state, permanently destroying their distinguishability. The optimal separating experiment is inspection, showing that cheap motion and useful information are different quantities.

The solver can also condition on a fixed action prefix while preserving its entire recorded sensor history. Calling `adaptive_experiment` with `belief=[0,1]` and `prefix=["reset"]` on the hidden-bit example makes guaranteed identification impossible. With `prefix=["inspect"]`, the remaining optimal cost is zero because the bit has already been learned, and total cost is three. With `prefix=["inspect", "reset"]`, remaining cost is still zero and total cost is four: erasing the device after observing its bit does not erase the observer's information. Beliefs with identical current states remain separated by their earlier observations when those observations already identify their different initial targets. Prefixes are capped at 512 actions, and the returned total cost includes that already executed prefix as well as the optimized remaining experiment.

The arithmetic bridge extends the same architecture to a genuinely unbounded original object. `pell_residue_system(D, unit, seed, modulus, accepted_x=...)` takes a supplied norm-one unit a+b√D and a seed on x²−Dy²=N. Multiplication uses the matrix [[a,Db],[b,a]], whose determinant is one and whose inverse replaces b with −b. Direct expansion proves preservation of x²−Dy²; its characteristic polynomial gives zₙ₊₂=2azₙ₊₁−zₙ for each coordinate. Reduction modulo an integer m produces an invertible finite action, so the seed's orbit is a closed cycle without a transient, even for composite m. Advance and retreat become ordinary Observatory actions, and index n maps to cycle index n modulo its period. The record states whether the supplied generator is fundamental, while its completeness claim remains restricted to the supplied seed and generator orbit.

For D=2, unit (3,2), seed (1,0) and modulus 35, the compiled cycle has period six. Requiring terminal x≡1 selects cycle index zero. Among 100-step advance/retreat words, exactly 422,550,200,076,076,467,165,567,735,126 are accepted. An independent check sums binomial(100,k) over (2k−100)≡0 modulo six, because a word with k advances has that net exponent. `pell_point` separately reaches signed indices by binary powering; at index 10¹⁰⁰ modulo 1,000,000,007 it returns (366654601,394441112), and the norm identity replays modulo that modulus. These results concern one seeded orbit. They do not establish that the seed covers all integer solutions of the norm equation; that additional arithmetic completeness problem must be handled separately.

Reproduce the retained computations with `python scripts/benchmark_observatory.py --horizon 80`, and run the Observatory suite with `python -m unittest discover -s tests -p "test_observatory*.py"`. The machine-readable interferometer record is `results/observatory/benchmark.json`. Independent comparisons include forward propagation in absolute, relative and sign coordinates, complete enumeration of all 8,191 binary words through length twelve for the modulus-five example, rank/inverse-rank round trips, basis-word replays, scalar polynomial gcd and remote matrix powering. Unit tests also compare random finite systems against direct enumeration, exercise repeated-root and nilpotent recurrences, check zero outputs and empty alphabets, and compare adaptive decisions with exhaustive small games. Timing fields describe the recorded workloads on their execution environment; they are not universal speedup claims.

The Pell and destructive-prefix records are saved separately as `results/observatory/pell.json` and `results/observatory/destructive-prefix.json`. After installing the package, the following Python snippet recreates both directly from their public APIs. It preserves the distinction between the initial unexecuted plan, resetting before learning and inspecting before any further action. The inspect-then-reset case is additionally covered by the experiment tests.

```python
import json
from pathlib import Path
from perfectjet.observatory.arithmetic import arithmetic_receipt
from perfectjet.observatory.examples import irreversible_machine
from perfectjet.observatory.experiments import adaptive_experiment

destination = Path("results/observatory")
destination.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
(destination / "pell.json").write_text(
    json.dumps(arithmetic_receipt(), indent=2) + "\n", encoding="utf-8")
prefixes = {"none": [], "reset": ["reset"], "inspect": ["inspect"]}
records = {name: adaptive_experiment(irreversible_machine(), belief=[0, 1], prefix=word)
           for name, word in prefixes.items()}
(destination / "destructive-prefix.json").write_text(
    json.dumps(records, indent=2) + "\n", encoding="utf-8")
```

The implemented resource boundaries are explicit. The workbench accepts at most 10,000 input states and sixteen actions. History queries allow horizons through 512 and at most one million dynamic states. Exact rational closure defaults to at most 128 quotient states. Adaptive exploration defaults to 4,096 beliefs and reports `limit`, with solvability unresolved, when it cannot finish within that allowance. Pell residue compilation defaults to at most 100,000 orbit states; unmodulated Pell coordinates have a one-million-bit allocation guard. Backend functions expose relevant limits where supported. The workbench asks for a modulus beyond length 10,000 to avoid enormous exact outputs. This release adds computational identities, proofs of the algorithms given here and replayable evidence; it adds no new Lean proof of these executions. It is a finite deterministic tool, with a seeded arithmetic adapter, rather than a general solver for arbitrary mathematics or noisy stochastic dynamics.

The next expansion should preserve this architecture while increasing the classes of admissible models. Existing `engine.adaptive_closure` and `polynomial.feature_closure` already describe polynomial dynamics through exact feature identities. A polynomial adapter should submit the user's requested outputs, generate a finite closed feature representation when one is found, and return the residual monomials when its degree or feature budget prevents closure. Existing `lattice.integer_solve`, `smith_form`, `hermite_form` and `saturation` can supply integral feasibility and transport checks, so rational coordinates do not get mistaken for realizable integer states. Existing `quadratic.QuadraticSquare` can contribute arithmetic solution populations beyond the seeded Pell demonstration, with modular filters accompanied by their precise lifting obligations. `parametric.rank_cells` can expose parameter regions where observability changes, and `readout.identify` can produce exact hidden-direction witnesses and sensor-repair questions.

A concrete next stage is therefore an adapter contract carrying the original model, the question, the compiled representation, its transport map, the retained readouts, identities, limits and unresolved obligations. `engine.report` already provides a related reporting vocabulary. The workbench can then offer a user's next mathematically justified action: refine a sensor, change an experiment after a reading, inspect an arithmetic obstruction, or evaluate a remotely indexed count. Those additional adapters require implementation and domain-specific completeness arguments; they are a development plan rather than a delivered universal interface. The current release supplies the shared executable nucleus: ordered dynamics, demand-specific compression, enormous exact populations, arithmetic transport and experiments that act on what their observations reveal.

# PerfectJet Math Lab: recovered mathematics in one executable workflow

PerfectJet 0.17.0 connects the strongest mathematical engines through one local interface and implements the recoveries identified in the October 16 equation assessment. The catalogue contains 464 stable equation entries organized into 24 ranked families. Twenty-five question-oriented tools are accessible from the browser, JSON and Python. The complete finite-system Observatory remains available in the same window. The new modules use the Python standard library; the optional historical NFL research dependencies remain separate.

The first 417 equation records retain their IDs and meanings. New entries PJ-E418 through PJ-E450 identify the recovered Moyal/Hahn gate, cyclic extension repair, sector connectivity, conductor transport, finite theta closure, cancellation control, weighted graph bounds, outcome-channel accounting, logarithmic periods, arithmetic character identities, positive Pell normalization, the archived quintic partner and the integrated decision workflow. Ranking is an explicit judgment of reusable mathematical reach. It does not convert a historical report, proposal, rejected formula or open converse into an implemented theorem. A representative tool link in the equation drawer launches a related family computation; it does not claim that every historical statement in that family is executable.

## Start with a question

Extract the complete archive and run `python run_observatory.py`, or double-click `Start_Math_Lab.bat` on Windows. The launcher starts the loopback server and opens the page. Python 3.10 or newer is required. The first view offers five plain-language questions: what can we know, what possibilities fit, what should we measure, is a calculation reliable, and what hidden structure explains the result. Each tool has a computed example, exact printed results, its mathematical scope, expandable details and a downloadable calculation record. Available numeric and Boolean inputs change the computation; advanced requests use the same JSON router as the CLI. The page retains all earlier finite-model editing, action-history animation, cost ceilings, sensor traces, rank navigation, adaptive policies and remote series queries behind the Finite systems switch.

The animation displays returned state counts, values, branches or other computed geometry. Bars are reserved for commensurate quantities; heterogeneous metrics use labeled flow nodes. Animation can be paused or replayed, and reduced-motion preferences are respected. A moving highlight does not represent new simulated observations. All 464 equation entries can be searched by ID, family, formula, evidence status or assumptions, and rejected entries remain labeled rejected.

For an installed checkout, `python -m pip install -e .` installs the core without optional scientific libraries. Run `perfectjet lab --open` for the interface. `perfectjet math-lab --lab precision` evaluates one tool, `perfectjet math-lab --request examples/mathlab/pipeline.json` evaluates an exported request, and `perfectjet math-lab --all --output results/mathlab/all-examples.json` recomputes every default example. The installed wheel includes the complete equation and ranking resources; an external repository path is not needed to read the atlas from an installed package.

```python
from perfectjet.mathlab import catalogue, evaluate

print(len(catalogue()["equations"]))
answer = evaluate({"operation": "math-lab", "lab": "pipeline",
                   "params": {"uncertainty": "1/4"}})
print(answer["summary"])
print(answer["metrics"])
```

## One complete decision rather than disconnected demonstrations

`workflow.decision_pipeline` accepts measurements $Hx=y$, one target loading $g_i$ per original item, nonnegative integer resource costs and capacities, and explicit deterministic observation and model bounds. It first checks whether every requested target is in the measured row space. When a target is hidden, it returns a nullspace witness and searches the supplied finite candidate sensors for a minimum-total-cost repair. It does not pretend that those extra measurements have already been obtained. An empty or inadequate candidate list returns an explicit absence of a repair within that list.

When recovery is possible, exact weights satisfy $g_i=w_i^T H$. The item value is $v_i=w_i^Ty$, and its conservative error radius is $r_i=\sum_j|w_{ij}|e_j+m_i$. A single `SelectionPopulation` then counts every feasible original portfolio, supplies its rank and inverse rank, and computes the nominal optimum with every tie. For each feasible selection $b$, the adapter computes $\hat J(b)=\sum_i b_i v_i$ and $r(b)=\sum_i b_i r_i$. A unique winner is certified only if its lower bound exceeds every rival upper bound. Shared observation errors make these simple radii conservative; interval overlap is not a proof that the underlying choice is impossible.

The default example has three items with resource vectors $(2,1),(1,2),(2,2)$, capacities $(3,3)$, measured latent values $(12,8)$ and target rows $(1,0),(0,1),(1,2)$. It has five feasible portfolios. The third item has value 28, while the first two together have value 20, giving an exact margin of eight at zero supplied error. Increasing the observation uncertainty can remove the certificate. Replacing the two measurements by their sum makes individual targets structurally hidden. These three cases distinguish a separated winner, uncertain contenders and missing information using the same input model.

The adapter permits at most fourteen original items, twelve latent coordinates, three resources and twelve candidate repair sensors. It enumerates all feasible portfolios after completion counting; it does not claim scalable arbitrary nonlinear optimization. Measurement centers must fit an exact nominal latent state, even when nonzero sensitivity bounds are supplied. General inconsistent noisy-center interval feasibility is outside this adapter. The stated bounds are caller-supplied deterministic assumptions, not fitted confidence intervals, live prices, buyer commitments or a claim of market or NFL superiority. Exact zero-error ties are reported as ties, with the optimal count and retained choices; requesting more arithmetic precision cannot break an exact tie.

## Exact Moyal and Hahn engines

`moyal.py` supplies Gaussian rational coefficients and multivariate polynomials over $\mathbb Q[i][\hbar]$. The finite star product computes $f\star g=\sum_k(i\hbar/2)^k\Lambda^k(f,g)/k!$. It also exposes Poisson brackets, the scaled Moyal commutator, Egorov defects, classification into exact, central-defect and noncentral-defect cases, polynomial pullbacks and covariance examples. Higher terms vanish when either polynomial has degree at most two. That affine-quadratic sector is a Lie subalgebra under the scaled commutator; it is not an associative star subalgebra.

For the retained convention, the cubic defect is $\Delta_\hbar(q^3,p^3)=-3\hbar^2/2$, while the quintic defect is $-150\hbar^2q^2p^2+15\hbar^4/2$. Ordinary affine symplectic substitution preserves the same flat star product. For the nonlinear symplectic shear $\Phi(q,p)=(q,p+q^2)$ and $f=p^2,g=p$, Poisson covariance holds but ordinary star covariance has exact defect $-\hbar^2/2$. Nonlinear covariance needs a transported product or corrected quantum pullback. The new module was adapted from the recovered source packet while removing its optional NumPy experiment from the supported exact core.

`hahn.py` computes the integer coefficients $S_k(a,b,c,d)=\sum_r(-1)^r\binom kr(a)_{k-r}(b)_r(c)_r(d)_{k-r}$. With $\kappa=\min(a,d)+\min(b,c)$, monomial exactness is equivalent to the vanishing of every odd $S_k$ from three through $\kappa$. The full finite gate is important: the decoy $(1,4,3r+2,2r)$ has $S_3=0$ but $S_5=720r^2(3r-1)(3r+1)(3r+2)\ne0$. The four classified $\kappa=4$ ray families and reversals are generated explicitly. The manuscript's global absence conjecture for further nonzero-Poisson corridors remains conjectural; a degree-80 census does not prove it. The tests compare the independent Hahn coefficient classifier against the exact polynomial Moyal engine and replay the classified finite sector.

## Constructive obstruction repair and reachable algebra

`categorical.py` represents cyclic phases as exact rational numbers modulo integers. It computes the cocycle $\omega_k(a,b,c)=\exp(2\pi i k a\lfloor(b+c)/n\rfloor/n)$, its class-detecting cyclic product, and the explicit cochain $B(a,b)=\exp(2\pi i k(a\bmod n)\lfloor b/n\rfloor/n)$ on $\mathbb Z/n^2$. The coboundary equation $dB=q^*\omega_k$ is replayed over every triple within the declared budget. A nontrivial class on the original cyclic group can therefore become explicitly trivial after this extension. The tool does not claim a minimum extension or an arbitrary-group repair theorem.

The same module accepts full matrix sectors and a rational self-adjoint projector. An edge joins sectors when the corresponding projector block is nonzero. The generated star algebra is a direct sum of full matrix algebras on the connected component sums, and is the full matrix algebra exactly when the sector graph is connected. Small examples independently generate the matrix algebra and compare its dimension with the graph result. Projector, symmetry and resource checks are explicit; mathematical access to a cross-sector operator is a supplied resource, not something a physical superselection rule automatically permits.

## Exact conductor transport, theta coefficients and torus products

`conductor_transport.py` implements unnormalized finite Fourier maps and chirps in exact cyclotomic quotient rings. For $M=dL$, support embedding and periodic pullback satisfy $F_M\iota_d=\pi^*F_L$, $F_M\pi^*=d\iota_dF_L$ and $T_M\iota_d=\iota_dT_L^d$. Coprime CRT factorization retains the required twists. These are maps between specified representations; a complement of an embedding is not automatically an invariant old/new subrepresentation. The normalized square-root factors are explained separately so they do not get mixed into the exact unnormalized implementation.

For an integer symmetric matrix $C$ with even diagonal, `FiniteLatticeTheta` computes $Q(a)=a^TCa/2$ and the finite numerator $N(x)=\sum_{\rho\in\{0,\ldots,L-1\}^r}\zeta^{Q(\rho)}x^{|\rho|}$. Residue periodicity gives the complete series $N(x)/(1-x^L)^r$. The implementation evaluates remote coefficients, compares short direct coefficients and verifies the boundary-twisted numerator reciprocity. It does not infer modularity of an associated infinite Euler product. The torus skein API computes root-of-unity products and commutators without silently reducing curve coordinates modulo the conductor. The half-lift API implements the integer-height parity criterion at even level, with the necessary normalization restriction retained.

## Precision, finite weighted populations and outcome channels

`precision.py` treats terms as exact rationals and replays round-to-nearest, ties-to-even at a declared significand precision with arbitrary exponent range. A float denotes its stored binary value; a decimal string denotes the exact declared decimal. For a nonzero sum, $\kappa_{sum}=\sum|t_j|/|\sum t_j|$ measures cancellation. The bound $\gamma_{N-1}\kappa_{sum}$ applies under its roundoff assumptions; zero sums use absolute bounds. Evaluation, input and model errors remain separate. The module can increase precision until a stated arithmetic tolerance is met, or decline a comparison whose external uncertainty remains too large. It reproduces $10^{16}+1-10^{16}=0$ at 53-bit recursive precision and repairs the sum to one at 106 bits. It does not model IEEE underflow, overflow or exception flags.

`weighted_growth.py` supplies exact type-histogram repricing, weighted moments and covariances, finite colored path counting, chronology-sensitive edge weights, and finite nonnegative-matrix row/block tail certificates. Floating log-weight evaluations are separately labeled. A block norm can establish contraction even when a one-step row bound fails. Transfer to an external infinite path population requires an explicit weight-domination contract. Submultiplicativity can provide an infimum characterization of a growth constant; it does not make every finite root bound monotonically decrease. These distinctions prevent a convenient finite calculation from being advertised as an unrelated infinite-model theorem.

`branch_channels.py` retains exact, frame-correctable, repeat and silent-failure outcome classes with probabilities summing exactly to one. Under the declared independent identical-attempt model, failure after $R$ attempts is $p_s\sum_{j=0}^{R-1}p_r^j+p_{exhaust}p_r^R$. Expected attempt cost, acceptance-conditioned error, exhaustion and the silent floor remain distinct. The scheduler checks every allowed finite depth against a strict failure threshold and optimizes the declared cost-plus-risk objective. Increasing repetition cannot eliminate the asymptotic silent floor $p_s/(1-p_r)$ when $p_r<1$. Correction operators and device probabilities are input assumptions, and no hardware performance claim is introduced.

## Analytic replacement and arithmetic reconstruction

`analytic_recovery.py` replaces a specific tracked circular logarithmic integral by its exact rational coefficient of $2\pi i$. It validates the supplied divisor geometry, charge, loop radius, orientation and branch convention. The resulting integral depends on the representative and radius; only the logarithmic charge is the robust integer datum. The caller remains responsible for the completeness of the supplied divisor list. This computation does not claim a radius-independent period or a knot-volume theorem.

The same module implements exact cyclotomic Dirichlet characters, finite coefficients of $\sum\varphi(n)\chi(n)n^{-s}=L(s-1,\chi)/L(s,\chi)$, independent formal Dirichlet division and reduced-progression character decomposition. Correct Euler factors are retained. In particular, the residue for $n\equiv1\pmod3$ is $3/(8\zeta(2))$, rather than the unqualified $1/\zeta(2)$ sometimes written in the older prose. Exact finite coefficient tests support the implementation; the standard analytic identity uses $\Re s>2$ before continuation. Irrational phase twists remain a separate analytic problem.

`algebra_bridges.py` makes the existing quadratic compiler, supplied Pell orbit, complete finite action-history population, bounded invariant-law search and parameter-rank/Smith analysis accessible through explicit adapters. Rank cells describe algebraic rank, while an integer solution is certified at a specified specialization; constant rank does not imply constant integer feasibility. The quadratic-history adapter checks original input reconstruction and nonnegative domains before accepting an endpoint, retaining original word multiplicity.

The signed Pell asymptotic issue is corrected in `quadratic.py`. Positive embedding classes are reduced exactly into a unit fundamental interval and deduplicated; admissible reconstruction residues supply an exact rational logarithmic weight. For $n^2+7=2y^2$, the correct positive-input coefficient is $2/\log(3+\sqrt8)$, and there are 31 hits through $10^{12}$. Summing four raw signed orbit records would double that coefficient. Raw orbit records are still inspectable, and exact hit enumeration remains intact. The seeded residue adapter continues to claim only its supplied seed/unit orbit, not every norm solution.

`recovered_partners.py` restores the archived squarefree quintic partner, its factored eliminant metadata and exact active-gate replay. It also exposes universal cubic/quartic and sufficient degree-12 sextic constructions through the existing covariant engine. The full maximal-minor elimination behind the archived eliminant is not regenerated by displaying its factors; rounded complex orbit matching is not an exact orbit proof; the unrestricted degree-12 converse stays open.

## Evidence and reproduction

Run the complete suite with `PYTHONPATH=src python -m unittest discover -s tests`. Run `python scripts/check_coverage.py`, `PYTHONPATH=src python scripts/build_mathlab.py --check`, and `PYTHONPATH=src python scripts/check_observatory_ui.py` to check document coverage, installed resource freshness and the actual HTTP/JavaScript interaction bank. The interface bank executes the page's JavaScript against a DOM contract and real HTTP results. It explicitly records that browser rendering is unverified when no browser executable is installed; a passing DOM bank is not a screenshot or a visual-render claim.

The package preserves all prior research directories and distinguishes mathematical computation from empirical validation. This release adds exact Python implementations, independent finite checks and replayable examples; it adds no new Lean proof of these executions. Its categorical, representation and analytic results retain their precise assumptions. Open global conjectures, arbitrary nonlinear closure, empirical scouting superiority and live market profitability are not silently closed by placing them beside a working engine.

## Sextic partner classification (0.17.0)

The existing partners card now includes exact incidence analysis. Submit `{"lab":"partners","params":{"operation":"incidence","degree":5}}` to compute a rational kernel, replay every basis vector's correction gates and distinguish active from commuting partners. A custom form uses the existing exact binary term array. Degrees 3–20 are accepted. The classification metadata is conditional on archived optional msolve/Singular results where stated; this request does not run those solvers. The original 450 stable equation records and all existing tools remain, with fourteen distinct research entries appended as PJ-E451–PJ-E464 in the existing ranked family. See PARTNER_INCIDENCE.md for assumptions and open obligations.

# Partner incidence over the full sextic space

The partner question asks, for a binary sextic f and a degree N, whether some nonzero form g of degree N satisfies the correction gates (f,g)_3 = (f,g)_5 = 0. The set P_N of sextics whose kernel ker L_N(f) exceeds its generic dimension is closed and GL2-invariant. Earlier releases studied P_N on two-parameter families. PR #2 (originally labelled 0.14.0), integrated in release 0.17.0, studies it in the full seven-dimensional space of sextics, which is the formal frontier this atlas has listed since 0.1.0.

## Two reductions that make the full space computable

**Lines find hypersurfaces.** A line f0 + t f1 meets every hypersurface component of P_N whose defining invariant does not vanish at f1, and a generic line misses every component of codimension two or more. Along the line, the exact one-parameter locus (`loci.rank_locus`) is therefore the restriction of the hypersurface part of P_N. Its degree is the degree of that hypersurface. Dividing invariant monomials of the matching weight by it identifies the defining invariant exactly (`incidence.identify_hypersurface`). On two independent random lines, only two degrees between 3 and 19 have a hypersurface component:
- degree 3, the quartic hypersurface A^2 = B/6;
- degree 12, the quartic hypersurface A^2 = 25B/96.

In both cases the restriction of the invariant equals the locus exactly, so on these lines the hypersurface part of P_N is that invariant hypersurface and nothing else. The tested lines find no hypersurface component in the other degrees. This alone does not establish a global exclusion.

**The slice finds everything else.** Every sextic with at least three simple roots is GL2-equivalent to xy(x-y)(x^3+px^2y+qxy^2+ry^3), with three simple roots at 0, 1 and infinity. Rank conditions on L_N become polynomial ideals in (p,q,r). Saturating by r and by 1+p+q+r removes placements where 0 or 1 is a repeated root. Those sextics reappear at other placements. When the locus is zero-dimensional, `incidence.classify_zero_dimensional` works in the finite algebra Q[p,q,r]/I:
- it computes the Clebsch invariants of the slice form with coefficients in that algebra (`clebsch_over`);
- it inverts A when A is a unit;
- it returns the exact minimal polynomials of the absolute invariants B/A^2, C/A^3 and D/A^5.

If each minimal polynomial is linear, every point of the locus has the same Clebsch point. When that point belongs to a squarefree sextic, the GIT fibre over it is a single orbit, so the locus is exactly one GL2-orbit. Sextics with fewer than three simple roots are, up to GL2, seven fixed forms and the family x^2y^2(x-y)(x-sy). They are checked directly (`degenerate_profile`).

## Classification in small degrees

**Degree 3.** For every sextic, det L_3 = (A^2-B/6)/16, certified in 0.9.0. On the slice, dim ker L_3 >= 2 is a zero-dimensional locus of ten points. Each point has absolute invariants (6,-36,0), which are those of x^6+y^6. Ten is 120 ordered placements divided by the order-12 dihedral stabilizer of the six roots. Among the degenerate forms, only those with a root of multiplicity at least five have a two-dimensional cubic kernel.

**Degree 4.** Every sextic has the quartic partner (f,f)_4, certified in 0.9.0. On the slice, dim ker L_4 >= 2 is zero-dimensional with all absolute invariants 0, which is the octahedral sextic xy(x^4-y^4). Among degenerate forms, only x^6 and x^5y have a larger quartic kernel. Hence dim ker L_4 >= 2 exactly when f is octahedral or has a root of multiplicity at least five.

**Degree 5.** The ideal of 6-by-6 minors of the 8-by-6 matrix L_5 on the slice is zero-dimensional of length 20. In Q[p,q,r]/I, B-486A^2, C+26244A^3 and D+419904A^5 reduce to zero and A is a unit. These are the absolute invariants of x^6+(40/7)x^3y^3+y^6, the C3 sextic at the 40/7 obstruction. Twenty is 120 placements divided by its order-6 stabilizer, so every point is a reduced point of that single orbit. The degenerate forms with a quintic partner are exactly those with a root of multiplicity at least four. These include x^4y(x-y), which is the GL2 class of (x-y)^4(x^2+4xy+y^2) at (+/-16,-9). Therefore:

> A binary sextic has a nonzero quintic g with (f,g)_3 = (f,g)_5 = 0 if and only if it has a root of multiplicity at least four, or it is GL2-equivalent over the algebraic closure to x^6 + (40/7) x^3 y^3 + y^6.

The 7lambda-40 obstruction, found by an ansatz in the earliest archive and rediscovered in 0.3.0, is therefore the only squarefree source of quintic partners. On the slice no sextic has two independent quintic partners.

**Degree 6.** Exact rational Groebner bases of the 6-by-6 minors of L_6 on the slice do not finish. `modular.py` computes the same ideal over a prime field instead:
- it builds all 1005 nonzero minors by fraction-free Bareiss modulo P;
- it interreduces them linearly, since polynomials of this degree in three variables span few monomials;
- it decides emptiness away from repeated roots at 0 and 1 with one Rabinowitsch variable.

Modulo 2147483629 and 1000000007 the locus is empty. A global numerical search on the slice agrees: about 1500 Gauss-Newton starts converge only to repeated-root forms. Among degenerate forms, a two-dimensional sextic kernel occurs exactly when there is a root of multiplicity at least four. With evidence modulo two primes rather than a rational proof:

> dim ker L_6(f) >= 2 if and only if f has a root of multiplicity at least four.

The engine is calibrated on degree 5. Modulo P it reports the quintic locus nonempty and the two-dimensional quintic locus empty, which matches the exact rational results above.

**Degree 7.** The 8-by-8 minors of the 12-by-8 matrix L_7 on the slice give 493 nonzero polynomials. Modulo 2147483629 and 1000000007, the locus is empty away from repeated roots at 0 and 1. A numerical search with 3000 starts converges only to repeated-root forms. Among degenerate forms, the septic kernel is nonzero exactly when there is a root of multiplicity at least four. With the same evidential status as degree 6:

> ker L_7(f) != 0 if and only if f has a root of multiplicity at least four.

Across degrees 5 to 7, then, a root of multiplicity at least four is the uniform source of extra partners. The C3 class at 40/7 in degree 5 is the only exception.

**Degree 8.** No line meets a hypersurface component, so P_8 has codimension at least two. The C3 family x^6+lambda x^3y^3+y^6 has the partner g_8 = xy(x^6+y^6-(7/20)lambda x^3y^3) for every lambda. Its gate coefficients are polynomials of degree at most two in lambda, and they vanish at five values (`c3_degree8_certificate`). On that family, the invariants satisfy exactly two relations of lowest weight (`c3_curve_relations`):

R5 = AB^2 + BC/6 - 9D/2 = 0 and C^2 = 6B^3.

These relations hold everywhere the degree-8 kernel is nonzero. On the slice, random projections det(R L_8) cut out a set containing the true locus. Modulo 2147483629 and 1000000007, the full three-variable radical test shows that R5 and C^2-6B^3 vanish on every point of it with no repeated root at 0 or 1. As a control, A does not vanish there. Plane sections meet the curve in 16 points, and on them both relations lie in the ideal itself. A global Gauss-Newton search gives the same answer: 557 squarefree degree-8 points on the slice all satisfy both relations to about 1e-14. The 26 points from three non-symmetric families satisfy them too.

The solutions of the two relations in weighted projective space form a single rational curve, parametrized by (A, B=6t^2, C=36t^3, D determined by R5). The C3 family maps onto it. Its special members are:
- (x^3-y^3)^2, the GL2 class of x^2y^2(x-y)^2;
- the octahedral sextic, at (lambda^2+50)^2 = 0;
- x^3y^3, at lambda = infinity.

Every sextic on that curve, apart from the boundary, is therefore GL2-equivalent to a member of the C3 family, which has an automorphism of order three. Among degenerate forms, the degree-8 kernel is nonzero exactly for those with a root of multiplicity at least four, and for x^3y^3 and x^2y^2(x-y)^2. Hence, with the forward direction resting on two primes:

> ker L_8(f) != 0 if and only if f has a root of multiplicity at least four or f has a projective automorphism of order three, that is, f is GL2-equivalent to x^6+lambda x^3y^3+y^6 for some lambda, or to x^3y^3.

This explains the earlier degree-8 observations. Order-three sextics are bielliptic, because D3 contains involutions, so the earlier bielliptic observation is a consequence. The lines b = 15 +/- 3a/2 satisfy R5 but not C^2 = 6B^3, which is why they carry no partner. The cubic drop curve on the symmetric surface satisfies both relations; it is a second parametrization of the same C3 curve. A sextic like x(x^5-y^5), whose B, C and D do not lie on the curve, has no degree-8 partner, as predicted.

**Degree 12.** The hypersurface part of P_12 is A^2 = 25B/96, where the covariant G of 0.9.0 is a partner. Lower-dimensional components of P_12 are not excluded by this method.

**Status of the preceding investigation.** These were the obligations before the optional solver computations described below. They remain obligations for an independent dependency-free rational proof, while the archived external-solver classification covers degrees 3–20.

## Archived external solver results and the classification through degree 20

Release 0.14.0 adds two external computer-algebra checks: `scripts/singular_partner_check.py` uses Singular over a prime field, and `scripts/msolve_partner_check.py` uses msolve over Q. They are optional verifiers; the dependency-free core does not need them.
- **The system.** For each degree N and kernel threshold k, a few random integer projections det(P L_N S) on the slice vanish wherever dim ker L_N >= k, so their common zero set contains the locus. A Rabinowitsch variable removes repeated roots at 0 and 1.
- **Emptiness.** msolve decides over Q whether that system has a complex solution. An empty superset means an empty locus.
- **Invariant relations.** Multiplying the Rabinowitsch polynomial by an invariant relation J tests whether J vanishes on every point of the locus.
- **Controls.** The control relation A fails wherever the locus is nonempty, as it must, and Singular modulo 2147483629 and 1000000007 agrees with every msolve verdict. The receipts are in `results/partner_incidence_solver_receipts.json`.

Points of the locus have three simple roots, so their invariants do not all vanish. In degrees 3, 4, 10 and 14 the relations give B = C = D = 0, or B = 6A^2 with C = -36A^3 and D = 0, and these force A to be nonzero. The Clebsch point is then that of the octahedral sextic or of x^6+y^6. Both are stable, so the GIT fibre over that point is a single GL2-orbit. Combined with the exact profiles of the degenerate root patterns (`degenerate_profile`) and the exact partners of the named sextics, this gives, over the algebraic closure, trusting the msolve computations:

| N | Extra correction-free partners occur exactly when f |
|---|---|
| 3 | satisfies B = 6A^2; dim ker >= 2 exactly for x^6+y^6 or a root of multiplicity >= 5 |
| 4 | (beyond (f,f)_4) is octahedral xy(x^4-y^4) or has a root of multiplicity >= 5 |
| 5 | has a root of multiplicity >= 4, or is equivalent to x^6+(40/7)x^3y^3+y^6 |
| 6 | (beyond f itself) has a root of multiplicity >= 4 |
| 7, 9, 11, 13, 15, 17, 19 | has a root of multiplicity >= 4 |
| 8 | has a root of multiplicity >= 4, or an automorphism of order three (x^6+lambda x^3y^3+y^6, or x^3y^3) |
| 10 | has a root of multiplicity >= 4, is x^3y^3, or is equivalent to x^6+y^6 |
| 12 | satisfies A^2 = 25B/96 (the hypersurface, which contains the degenerate cases) |
| 14 | has a root of multiplicity >= 4, is x^3y^3, or is octahedral |
| 16, 18, 20 | has a root of multiplicity >= 4, or is x^3y^3 |

Every exceptional class beyond a fourfold root is a sextic with extra symmetry:
- the dihedral sextic x^6+y^6, in degrees 3 and 10;
- the octahedral sextic, in degrees 4 and 14;
- the order-three family, in degree 8;
- its 40/7 member, in degree 5.

The only other exceptions are the two invariant hypersurfaces in degrees 3 and 12. This is a computer-assisted classification: its rational steps rest on msolve's correctness, and degrees 6 to 8 also have the independent dependency-free modular evidence above.

## Scope

The line method is exact on each line it uses. That the lines are generic is supported by agreement between independent random lines; the method does not prove genericity. The slice computations are exact, and the orbit identifications rest on the classical fact that Clebsch invariants separate orbits of squarefree sextics. Degrees 6 to 20 are settled over Q by msolve (an external solver) and modulo two primes by Singular and the dependency-free engine. Degrees 21 and above remain open.

## Integration and trust boundary (17 October 2026)

All statements concern nonzero sextics over the algebraic closure of Q. Exact rational instance kernels and their third, fifth and first transvectants can now be evaluated through Math Lab's existing partners tool with operation `incidence`, an optional exact term-array `form`, and integer `degree` from 3 through 20. A kernel vector is correction-free; activity additionally requires a nonzero first transvectant. The default Math Lab partner result includes this analysis alongside the preserved recovered constructions.

The optional solver receipts are preserved byte for byte from PR #2. Their recorded verdicts are external computational evidence, not newly executed checks or Lean certificates. Finite-prime emptiness alone does not prove characteristic-zero emptiness. Lines establish exact restrictions, not certified genericity. The archived classification through degree 20 relies on msolve where stated. Degrees 21 and above, independent rational proof-carrying replay, and formal verification remain unresolved. Old catalogue entries retain their historical evidence status; new entries and the PR-ID mapping are in data/incidence_equations.json, without reusing any existing ID.

# Exact research frontiers: arithmetic symmetry, finite spectra and all-degree partners

**Research checkpoint, 20 October 2026 UTC.** This chapter adds 23 equations to the 675-record source catalogue without changing any preceding record. They stand as mathematical research: exact correspondences, finite uniqueness certificates, structural obstructions and all-degree boundary classifications. The historical literature already contains cyclotomic E8 lattices, Coxeter centralizer results and finite moment reconstruction methods. No worldwide priority, new Lean theorem, classifier improvement or enlarged Math Lab tool count is asserted here.

## Arithmetic information beyond the Coxeter spectrum

Let \(K=\mathbb Q(\zeta)\), \(\zeta=\zeta_{30}\), \(O=\mathbb Z[\zeta]\), and let \(C,A\) be the supplied E8 Coxeter and Cartan matrices. The integral Krylov basis \(B=(v,Cv,\ldots,C^7v)\), with \(v\) the eighth simple root, satisfies \(CB=BD\) for multiplication by \(\zeta\), and \(G=B^TAB\). The actual bilinear form has the arithmetic description

\[
G_{ij}=\operatorname{Tr}_{K/\mathbb Q}(\alpha\zeta^{i-j}),\qquad
\alpha=\frac{5-\zeta-2\zeta^2-2\zeta^3-\zeta^4+\zeta^6+2\zeta^7}{15}.
\]

These are finite rational identities, not a match of complex eigenvalues. The element \(\alpha\) is real and totally positive, with \(N(\alpha)=1/1265625\), \(\operatorname{disc}(K)=1265625\) and \(\det G=1\). Consequently \(\alpha O\) is the codifferent: the trace pairing remains integral and self-dual despite the displayed denominator. This arithmetic metric is information that a bare spectrum does not contain.

An integral map commuting with \(C\) is fixed by its value at 1 in the cyclic basis and equals multiplication by an element of \(O\). Thus the integral commutant is \(O\). A commuting map is an isometry exactly when \(u\bar u=1\). Every conjugate then has modulus one; Kronecker's theorem makes \(u\) a root of unity, and the roots of unity in \(K\) are \(\mu_{30}\). Therefore the isometry centralizer is exactly \(\langle C\rangle\), of order 30. This recovers a classical centralizer result in explicit coordinates.

A normalizing map with \(UC=C^aU\), \(a\in(\mathbb Z/30)^\times\), has the semilinear form \(x\mapsto u_a\sigma_a(x)\), \(\sigma_a(\zeta)=\zeta^a\). Trace nondegeneracy proves the precise isometry condition

\[
\alpha u_a\bar u_a=\sigma_a(\alpha).
\]

The direction of the norm ratio is essential. An arbitrary field automorphism need not preserve this metric. To find every integral lift, enumerate the 240 actual roots \(r\) and set

\[
K_a(r)=(r,C^ar,\ldots,C^{7a}r),\qquad
U=K_a(r)B^{-1},\qquad K_a(r)^TAK_a(r)=G.
\]

The complete finite enumeration yields 30 lifts for each of the eight unit exponents. Completeness follows because an isometry sends \(v\) to a root, and that root image fixes every column of an intertwiner in the cyclic basis. Hence the normalizer \(N\) has order 240 and fits into

\[
1\longrightarrow C_{30}\longrightarrow N\longrightarrow(\mathbb Z/30)^\times\longrightarrow1.
\]

This extension is **nonsplit**. An explicit lift of7 is

\[
S(x)=(\zeta^2+\zeta^3+\zeta^4+\zeta^5-\zeta^7)\sigma_7(x),
\qquad SC=C^7S,\quad S^4=C^{25}.
\]

Every other lift of7 is \(C^iS\), and

\[
(C^iS)^4=C^{25+10i}\ne I.
\]

The quotient element7 has order four, but no lift has fourth power one. A section would have to provide such a lift, which is impossible. The normalizer has two root orbits of size 120; its 240 elements are not identified with the 240 roots by cardinality. Complete proofs, conventions and exact replay belong to the [trace-form and normalizer account](https://github.com/towre676-cloud/perfectpower/blob/main/docs/COXETER_TRACE_NORMALIZER.md). These statements are PJ-E465–PJ-E470.

## A finite and sharp spectral certificate

For the existing Raghava coordinates define ordinary positive moments

\[
\mu_j=\frac{(2j)!}{4^j}r_j=\sum_q w_qx_q^j,
\qquad x_q=(q-\tfrac12)^2,\qquad\mu_0=r_0.
\]

Here \(0\le q<1\) and \(w_q\ge0\). Equal folded nodes are combined. For an \(s\)-node positive measure, the Hankel matrix \(H_{ij}=\mu_{i+j}\), \(0\le i,j<s\), is positive definite: its quadratic form is a positive sum of squares of a polynomial of degree below \(s\), which cannot vanish at all \(s\) distinct nodes unless it is zero. Solve

\[
H(p_0,\ldots,p_{s-1})^T=-(\mu_s,\ldots,\mu_{2s-1})^T
\]

and put \(p(X)=X^s+\sum_{j<s}p_jX^j\). The first \(2s\) coordinates determine this monic annihilator. The extra coordinate \(\mu_{2s}\) supplies the global certificate

\[
\sum_q w_qp(x_q)^2=0.
\]

Every nonnegative competitor with the same \(\mu_0,\ldots,\mu_{2s}\) must therefore be supported on the roots of \(p\). Vandermonde inversion fixes the weights. No conductor bound or bound on the competing support size is assumed. Signed competitors would invalidate the square argument. Once recovered, \(p\) gives every subsequent moment by \(\mu_{n+s}+\sum_{j<s}p_j\mu_{n+j}=0\).

For E8 reflection the folded nodes are \(1,4,16,49\), divided by 225, each with weight 2. Thus **mass and the first eight Raghava moments** certify the folded spectrum among every nonnegative finite phase profile. Nine coordinates including mass suffice, without restricting competitors to conductor 30.

The count is sharp among positive rational weighted profiles. Take the increasing nodes

\[
0,\ 1/225,\ 1/100,\ 4/225,\ 1/25,\ 16/225,\ 1/9,\ 49/225,\ 1/4,
\qquad\delta_i=\frac1{\prod_{j\ne i}(x_i-x_j)}.
\]

Comparing the degree-eight coefficient in Lagrange interpolation gives \(\sum_i\delta_ix_i^k=0\) for \(0\le k<8\), and1 at \(k=8\). With

\[
\varepsilon=\frac{14144}{3243658447265625}
=\min_{i\text{ odd}}\frac1{-\delta_i},\qquad
w'_i=2\mathbf1_{i\text{ odd}}+\varepsilon\delta_i,
\]

all nine weights are positive. All offsets are rational and the corresponding symmetric phases have conductor dividing30. This profile has mass8 and matches \(r_0,\ldots,r_7\), but differs in \(\mu_8\) by \(\varepsilon\). The weights are rational, so minimality among actual dimension-eight integer representations is not established.

Even coordinates lose orientation between \(q\) and \(1-q\). Restore it with absolutely convergent odd Mellin observations, at weights at least three:

\[
y_k=\pi^{-(2k+1)}\operatorname{Im}\sum_{n\ge1}\frac{\operatorname{Tr}(T^n)}{n^{2k+1}}
=\frac{(-1)^{k+1}2^{2k}}{(2k+1)!}\sum_qw_qB_{2k+1}(q),\quad k\ge1.
\]

The Bernoulli factorization \(B_{2k+1}(1/2+t)=t(t^2-1/4)P_{k-1}(t^2)\), with \(P_{k-1}\) monic, gives a scaled Vandermonde system on the interior folded nodes. If there are \(s_*\) such nodes, \(s_*\) odd rows recover their pair-weight differences; the two self-dual endpoints already have no orientation ambiguity. Reflection has four interior nodes, so nine even coordinates plus odd weights3,5,7,9 suffice. The adjoint has 16 folded nodes and 14 interior nodes, so 33 even and 14 odd coordinates suffice. These are spectral conclusions, not recovery of the integral metric or Lie bracket. See the [finite reconstruction proof](https://github.com/towre676-cloud/perfectpower/blob/main/docs/COXETER_RECONSTRUCTION.md). These statements are PJ-E471–PJ-E477.

## All-degree sextic structure from local polynomial identities

Let \(K_N(f)=\{g\in S_N:(f,g)_3=(f,g)_5=0\}\), with nonzero binary sextic \(f\), characteristic zero and \(N\ge3\). Activity additionally requires \((f,g)_1\ne0\). With \(f=y^6F(t)\), \(g=y^NG(t)\), \(t=x/y\), the unnormalized affine gate is

\[
(f,g)_r\big|_{y=1}=\sum_{i=0}^r(-1)^i\binom ri
(6-r+i)_i(N-i)_{r-i}F^{(r-i)}G^{(i)}.
\]

Falling factorials are used. Verification on pure powers proves the identity by bilinearity because pure powers span in characteristic zero. The third equation is

\[
-120FG'''+60(N-2)F'G''-12(N-1)(N-2)F''G'+N(N-1)(N-2)F'''G=0.
\]

At a point of exact multiplicity \(k\) in \(f\), write \(F=at^k+\cdots\), \(G=bt^s+\cdots\), \(ab\ne0\). The leading gate coefficient is

\[
I_{r,k}(N,s)=\sum_i(-1)^i\binom ri(6-r+i)_i(N-i)_{r-i}(k)_{r-i}(s)_i.
\]

Both \(I_{3,k}\) and \(I_{5,k}\) must vanish. Remove their displayed common factors in \(s\). The remaining resultants are exact polynomials in \(N\): for \(k=1,5\) they are respectively \(-4(N-4)(N-2)(N-1)\) and its negative; for \(k=2,4\), \(-(N-6)(N-2)^3(N-1)^3(N+8)\); for \(k=3\), \((N-12)^2(N-2)^2(N-1)^4\); and for \(k=0,6\),1. These identities, not finite-degree extrapolation, prove for all \(N\ge13\):

| Exact multiplicity \(k\) of \(f\) | Allowed valuation \(s\) of a nonzero partner |
|---|---|
|0|0,1,2|
|1|0,1|
|2|0|
|3|\(N/2\), requiring even \(N\)|
|4|\(N\)|
|5|\(N-1,N\)|
|6|\(N-2,N-1,N\)|

For exact multiplicity \(k=4,5,6\), write \(f=\ell^kh\), \(\ell\nmid h\). The complete partner space is

\[
K_N(f)=\ell^{N-k+4}S_{k-4},\qquad\dim K_N(f)=k-3.
\]

The respective ranges are \(N\ge7,5,3\). Necessity follows from the local resultants. For sufficiency choose \(\ell=x\): the transverse degrees of \(f\) and every proposed partner sum to at most 2, so every third or fifth transvectant term differentiates more times transversely than the inputs allow. This proves a whole unbounded-degree stratum, rather than checking additional examples.

A particularly clean geometric equivalence holds for every \(N\ge3\):

\[
\ell^N\in K_N(f)\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\ell^4\mid f.
\]

After \(\ell=x\), the third gate is \(-(N)_3x^{N-3}\partial_y^3f\). Its vanishing is exactly the fourfold-root condition; the fifth gate then vanishes too. Its first gate is \(-Nx^{N-1}\partial_yf\), so the pure-power partner is active precisely when \(f\) is not proportional to \(\ell^6\).

Finite jet evaluation is injective: at a regular point a nonzero solution cannot vanish to order 3, so \(\dim K_N(f)\le3\). A simple root gives \(\dim K_N(f)\le2\) for \(N\ge5\); a double root gives \(\dim K_N(f)\le1\) for \(N\ge7\). A triple root excludes every odd \(N\ge13\). For two independent triple-root factors,

\[
K_N((\ell m)^3)=\operatorname{span}((\ell m)^{N/2})\quad(N\ge14\text{ even}),
\]

and it is zero for odd \(N\ge13\). The even partner is inactive. Divisibility at both roots proves necessity; cancellation of paired odd-transvectant terms proves sufficiency.

Finally, monomial sextics have complete dimension series \(H_f(t)=\sum_{N\ge3}\dim K_N(f)t^N\):

\[
H_{x^6}=\frac{3t^3}{1-t},\quad
H_{x^5y}=\frac{2t^3}{1-t}+t^4,\quad
H_{x^4y^2}=\frac{t^3}{1-t}+t^6,\quad
H_{x^3y^3}=\frac{t^4}{1-t^2}+2t^{12}.
\]

Each monomial partner has distinct gate output exponents, so common indicial roots give the full basis, including exceptional degrees 4,6,12. No graded-algebra or module structure is asserted for the kernels. Full factors, resultant certificates, thresholds and exact replay appear in the [partner structure proof](../PARTNER_STRUCTURE.md). These statements are PJ-E478–PJ-E487. They do not claim a global squarefree classification in degree 21 and above and do not upgrade the trust status of the archived external-solver classification.

## Research consequence and evidence boundaries

The spectral question now has a finite positive uniqueness theorem, a sharp counterexample and an orientation inverse. The lattice question retains the arithmetic form and a nonsplit symmetry extension. The sextic question now has exact local obstructions in every degree, complete high-multiplicity strata and complete two-root dimension series. These are complementary mathematical advances with their own standards of success.

The next serious targets are an explicit E8-to-H4 arithmetic restriction of scalars over the golden subfield, carrying the metric and lifts, and a global finite-type or differential-operator-gcd theorem for squarefree sextic partners. More finite searches would remain evidence until they supplied the missing all-degree argument. The chapter supplies exact solved boundary cases with which any such global theorem must agree.

The catalogue stores these claims as `derived`, with explicit assumptions and proof links. Exact Python checks accompany their separate implementation receipts; they are not Lean kernel formalization. The 23 new rows are present in the installed searchable equation resources, while the existing 29 Math Lab tools retain their previous supported parameter ranges. Every preceding 675 record and all 27 family ranks are preserved.
